Custom action head for continuous actions\\n            action, _ = vla.predict_action(\\n                **inputs,\\n                unnorm_key=cfg.unnorm_key,\\n                do_sample=False,\\n                proprio=proprio,\\n                proprio_projector=proprio_projector,\\n                noisy_action_projector=noisy_action_projector,\\n                action_head=action_head,\\n                use_film=use_film,\\n            )\\n\\n    # Return action chunk as list of actions\\n    return [action[i] for i in range(len(action))]\\n\\n\\ndef get_action_from_server(\\n    observation: Dict[str, Any], server_endpoint: str = \\\"http://0.0.0.0:8777/act\\\"\\n) -> Dict[str, Any]:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    Get VLA action from remote inference server.\\n\\n    Args:\\n        observation: Observation data to send to server\\n        server_endpoint: URL of the inference server\\n\\n    Returns:\\n        Dict[str, Any]: Action response from server\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    response = requests.post(\\n        server_endpoint,\\n        json=observation,\\n    )\\n    return response.json()\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.51}}"}

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:

After a previous dry-run produced 0% SR, the user's instruction was: "如果 SR = 0% → 不要提交完整 eval。详细记录每个 episode 的 action 输出值范围、机器人行为描述，汇报差异分析。" The current session continued diagnosing and fixing root causes, wrote a full eval script, ran a dry-run, got 0% SR again, and began investigating why.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **OpenVLA-OFT**: VLA model with L1 regression action head, K=8 action chunking, 7D actions, 8D proprio
   - **DinoSigLIP fused vision backbone**: Dual encoder, pixel_values (B,12,224,224) for 2 images, 256 patches/image
   - **External modules**: `action_head` (L1RegressionActionHead, 151.1M params) and `proprio_projector` (ProprioProjector, 16.8M params) loaded from separate .pt checkpoint files
   - **BOUNDS_Q99 normalization**: q01/q99 percentiles with mask-based selective normalization
   - **Image preprocessing**: 180° rotation (`[::-1, ::-1]`), TF JPEG roundtrip + lanczos3 resize, center crop (crop_scale=0.9)
   - **Proprio**: eef_pos(3D) + quat2axisangle(3D) + gripper_qpos(2D) = 8D
   - **Gripper post-processing**: normalize_gripper_action (binarize [0,1]→{-1,+1}) + invert_gripper_action (flip sign)
   - **2-image input**: agentview + wrist camera concatenated as pixel_values dim=1
   - **Action chunking**: deque(maxlen=8), execute all 8 actions before requerying model
   - **LIBERO eval parameters**: max_steps=280 (libero_object), num_steps_wait=10, env.seed(0), dummy_action=[0,0,0,0,0,0,-1]
   - **Task prompt**: Uses `task.language` (spaces) NOT `task.name` (underscores)

{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"def get_vla_action(\\n    cfg: Any,\\n    vla: torch.nn.Module,\\n    processor: Any,\\n    obs: Dict[str, Any],\\n    task_label: str,\\n    action_head: Optional[torch.nn.Module] = None,\\n    proprio_projector: Optional[torch.nn.Module] = None,\\n    noisy_action_projector: Optional[torch.nn.Module] = None,\\n    use_film: bool = False,\\n) -> List[np.ndarray]:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    Generate action predictions with the VLA policy.\\n\\n    Args:\\n        cfg: Configuration object with parameters\\n        vla: The VLA model\\n        processor: Model processor for inputs\\n        obs: Observation dictionary\\n        task_label: Text description of the task\\n        action_head: Optional action head for continuous actions\\n        proprio_projector: Optional proprioception projector\\n        noisy_action_projector: Optional noisy action projector for diffusion\\n        use_film: Whether to use FiLM\\n\\n    Returns:\\n        List[np.ndarray]: Predicted actions\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    with torch.inference_mode():\\n\\n        # Collect all input images\\n        all_images = [obs[\\\"full_image\\\"]]\\n        if cfg.num_images_in_input > 1:\\n            all_images.extend([obs[k] for k in obs.keys() if \\\"wrist\\\" in k])\\n\\n        # Process images\\n        all_images = prepare_images_for_vla(all_images, cfg)\\n\\n        # Extract primary image and additional images\\n        primary_image = all_images.pop(0)\\n\\n        # Build VLA prompt\\n        prompt = f\\\"In: What action should the robot take to {task_label.lower()}?\\\\nOut:\\\"\\n\\n        # Process primary image\\n        inputs = processor(prompt, primary_image).to(DEVICE, dtype=torch.bfloat16)\\n\\n        # Process additional wrist images if any\\n        if all_images:\\n            all_wrist_inputs = [\\n                processor(prompt, image_wrist).to(DEVICE, dtype=torch.bfloat16) for image_wrist in all_images\\n            ]\\n            # Concatenate all images\\n            primary_pixel_values = inputs[\\\"pixel_values\\\"]\\n            all_wrist_pixel_values = [wrist_inputs[\\\"pixel_values\\\"] for wrist_inputs in all_wrist_inputs]\\n            inputs[\\\"pixel_values\\\"] = torch.cat([primary_pixel_values] + all_wrist_pixel_values, dim=1)\\n\\n        # Process proprioception data if used\\n        proprio = None\\n        if cfg.use_proprio:\\n            proprio = obs[\\\"state\\\"]\\n            proprio_norm_stats = vla.norm_stats[cfg.unnorm_key][\\\"proprio\\\"]\\n            obs[\\\"state\\\"] = normalize_proprio(proprio, proprio_norm_stats)\\n            proprio = obs[\\\"state\\\"]\\n\\n        # Generate action\\n        if action_head is None:\\n            # Standard VLA output (single-image inputs, discrete actions)\\n            action, _ = vla.predict_action(**inputs, unnorm_key=cfg.unnorm_key, do_sample=False)\\n        else:\\n            # Custom action head for continuous actions\\n            action, _ = vla.predict_action(\\n                **inputs,\\n                unnorm_key=cfg.unnorm_key,\\n                do_sample=False,\\n                proprio=proprio,\\n                proprio_projector=proprio_projector,\\n                noisy_action_projector=noisy_action_projector,\\n                action_head=action_head,\\n                use_film=use_film,\\n            )\\n\\n    # Return action chunk as list of actions\\n    return [action[i] for i in range(len(action))]\\n\\n\\ndef get_action_from_server(\\n    observation: Dict[str, Any], server_endpoint: str = \\\"http://0.0.0.0:8777/act\\\"\\n) -> Dict[str, Any]:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    Get VLA action from remote inference server.\\n\\n    Args:\\n        observation: Observation data to send to server\\n        server_endpoint: URL of the inference server\\n\\n    Returns:\\n        Dict[str, Any]: Action response from server\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    response = requests.post(\\n        server_endpoint,\\n        json=observation,\\n    )\\n    return response.json()\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.44}}"}

{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"def prepare_observation(obs, resize_size):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Prepare observation for policy input.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    # Get preprocessed images\\n    img = get_libero_image(obs)\\n    wrist_img = get_libero_wrist_image(obs)\\n\\n    # Resize images to size expected by model\\n    img_resized = resize_image_for_policy(img, resize_size)\\n    wrist_img_resized = resize_image_for_policy(wrist_img, resize_size)\\n\\n    # Prepare observations dict\\n    observation = {\\n        \\\"full_image\\\": img_resized,\\n        \\\"wrist_image\\\": wrist_img_resized,\\n        \\\"state\\\": np.concatenate(\\n            (obs[\\\"robot0_eef_pos\\\"], quat2axisangle(obs[\\\"robot0_eef_quat\\\"]), obs[\\\"robot0_gripper_qpos\\\"])\\n        ),\\n    }\\n\\n    return observation, img  # Return both processed observation and original image for replay\\n\\n\\ndef process_action(action, model_family):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Process action before sending to environment.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    # Normalize gripper action [0,1] -> [-1,+1] because the environment expects the latter\\n    action = normalize_gripper_action(action, binarize=True)\\n\\n    # [OpenVLA] The dataloader flips the sign of the gripper action to align with other datasets\\n    # (0 = close, 1 = open), so flip it back (-1 = open, +1 = close) before executing the action\\n    if model_family == \\\"openvla\\\":\\n        action = invert_gripper_action(action)\\n\\n    return action\\n\\n\\ndef run_episode(\\n    cfg: GenerateConfig,\\n    env,\\n    task_description: str,\\n    model,\\n    resize_size,\\n    processor=None,\\n    action_head=None,\\n    proprio_projector=None,\\n    noisy_action_projector=None,\\n    initial_state=None,\\n    log_file=None,\\n):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Run a single episode in the environment.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    # Reset environment\\n    env.reset()\\n\\n    # Set initial state if provided\\n    if initial_state is not None:\\n        obs = env.set_init_state(initial_state)\\n    else:\\n        obs = env.get_observation()\\n\\n    # Initialize action queue\\n    if cfg.num_open_loop_steps != NUM_ACTIONS_CHUNK:\\n        print(f\\\"WARNING: cfg.num_open_loop_steps ({cfg.num_open_loop_steps}) does not match the NUM_ACTIONS_CHUNK \\\"\\n              f\\\"({NUM_ACTIONS_CHUNK}) constant defined in prismatic.vla.constants! For best performance (in terms of \\\"\\n               \\\"both speed and success rate), we recommend executing the full action chunk.\\\")\\n    action_queue = deque(maxlen=cfg.num_open_loop_steps)\\n\\n    # Setup\\n    t = 0\\n    replay_images = []\\n    max_steps = TASK_MAX_STEPS[cfg.task_suite_name]\\n\\n    # Run episode\\n    success = False\\n    try:\\n        while t < max_steps + cfg.num_steps_wait:\\n            # Do nothing for the first few timesteps to let objects stabilize\\n            if t < cfg.num_steps_wait:\\n                obs, reward, done, info = env.step(get_libero_dummy_action(cfg.model_family))\\n                t += 1\\n                continue\\n\\n            # Prepare observation\\n            observation, img = prepare_observation(obs, resize_size)\\n            replay_images.append(img)\\n\\n            # If action queue is empty, requery model\\n            if len(action_queue) == 0:\\n                # Query model to get action\\n                actions = get_action(\\n                    cfg,\\n                    model,\\n                    observation,\\n                    task_description,\\n                    processor=processor,\\n                    action_head=action_head,\\n                    proprio_projector=proprio_projector,\\n                    noisy_action_projector=noisy_action_projector,\\n                    use_film=cfg.use_film,\\n                )\\n                action_queue.extend(actions)\\n\\n            # Get action from queue\\n            action = action_queue.popleft()\\n\\n            # Process action\\n            action = process_action(action, cfg.model_family)\\n\\n            # Execute action in environment\\n            obs, reward, done, info = env.step(action.tolist())\\n            if done:\\n                success = True\\n                break\\n            t += 1\\n\\n    except Exception as e:\\n        log_message(f\\\"Episode error: {e}\\\", log_file)\\n\\n    return success, replay_images\\n\\n\\ndef run_t\\n\\n... Run episode\\n        success, replay_images = run_episode(\\n            cfg,\\n            env,\\n            task_description,\\n            model,\\n            resize_size,\\n            processor,\\n            action_head,\\n            proprio_projector,\\n            noisy_action_projector,\\n            initial_state,\\n            log_file,\\n        )\\n\\n        # Update counters\\n        task_episodes += 1\\n        total_episodes += 1\\n        if success:\\n            task_successes += 1\\n            total_successes += 1\\n\\n        # Save replay video\\n        save_rollout_video(\\n            replay_images, total_episodes, success=success, task_description=task_description, log_file=log_file\\n        )\\n\\n        # Log results\\n        log_message(f\\\"Success: {success}\\\", log_file)\\n        log_message(f\\\"# episodes completed so far: {total_episodes}\\\", log_file)\\n        log_message(f\\\"# successes: {total_successes} ({total_successes / total_episodes * 100:.1f}%)\\\", log_file)\\n\\n    # Log task results\\n    task_success_rate = float(task_successes) / float(task_episodes) if task_episodes > 0 else 0\\n    total_success_rate = float(total_successes) / float(total_episodes) if total_episodes > 0 else 0\\n\\n    log_message(f\\\"Current task success rate: {task_success_rate}\\\", log_file)\\n    log_message(f\\\"Current total success rate: {total_success_rate}\\\", log_file)\\n\\n    # Log to wandb if enabled\\n    if cfg.use_wandb:\\n        wandb.log(\\n            {\\n                f\\\"success_rate/{task_description}\\\": task_success_rate,\\n                f\\\"num_episodes/{task_description}\\\": task_episodes,\\n            }\\n        )\\n\\n    return total_episodes, total_successes\\n\\n\\n@draccus.wrap()\\ndef eval_libero(cfg: GenerateConfig) -> float:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Main function to evaluate a trained policy on LIBERO benchmark tasks.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    # Validate configuration\\n    validate_config(cfg)\\n\\n    # Set random seed\\n    set_seed_everywhere(cfg.seed)\\n\\n    # Initialize model and components\\n    model, action_head, proprio_projector, noisy_action_projector, processor = initialize_model(cfg)\\n\\n    # Get expected image dimensions\\n    resize_size = get_image_resize_size(cfg)\\n\\n    # Setup logging\\n    log_file, local_log_filepath, run_id = setup_logging(cfg)\\n\\n    # Initialize LIBERO task suite\\n    benchmark_dict = benchmark.get_benchmark_dict()\\n    task_suite = benchmark_dict[cfg.task_suite_name]()\\n    num_tasks = task_suite.n_tasks\\n\\n    log_message(f\\\"Task suite: {cfg.task_suite_name}\\\", log_file)\\n\\n    # Start evaluation\\n    total_episodes, total_successes = 0, 0\\n    for task_id in tqdm.tqdm(range(num_tasks)):\\n        total_episodes, total_successes = run_task(\\n            cfg,\\n            task_suite,\\n            task_id,\\n            model,\\n            resize_size,\\n            processor,\\n            action_head,\\n            proprio_projector,\\n            noisy_action_projector,\\n            total_episodes,\\n            total_successes,\\n            log_file,\\n        )\\n\\n    # Calculate final success rate\\n    final_success_rate = float(total_successes) / float(total_episodes) if total_episodes > 0 else 0\\n\\n    # Log final results\\n    log_message(\\\"Final results:\\\", log_file)\\n    log_message(f\\\"Total episodes: {total_episodes}\\\", log_file)\\n    log_message(f\\\"Total successes: {total_successes}\\\", log_file)\\n    log_message(f\\\"Overall success rate: {final_success_rate:.4f} ({final_success_rate * 100:.1f}%)\\\", log_file)\\n\\n    # Log to wandb if enabled\\n    if cfg.use_wandb:\\n        wandb.log(\\n            {\\n                \\\"success_rate/total\\\": final_success_rate,\\n                \\\"num_episodes/total\\\": total_episodes,\\n            }\\n        )\\n        wandb.save(local_log_filepath)\\n\\n    # Close log file\\n    if log_file:\\n        log_file.close()\\n\\n    return final_success_rate\\n\\n\\nif __name__ == \\\"__main__\\\":\\n    eval_libero()\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.44}}"}

Resize images to size expected by model\\n    img_resized = resize_image_for_policy(img, resize_size)\\n    wrist_img_resized = resize_image_for_policy(wrist_img, resize_size)\\n\\n    # Prepare observations dict\\n    observation = {\\n        \\\"full_image\\\": img_resized,\\n        \\\"wrist_image\\\": wrist_img_resized,\\n        \\\"state\\\": np.concatenate(\\n            (obs[\\\"robot0_eef_pos\\\"], quat2axisangle(obs[\\\"robot0_eef_quat\\\"]), obs[\\\"robot0_gripper_qpos\\\"])\\n        ),\\n    }\\n\\n    return observation, img  # Return both processed observation and original image for replay\\n\\n\\ndef process_action(action, model_family):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Process action before sending to environment.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    # Normalize gripper action [0,1] -> [-1,+1] because the environment expects the latter\\n    action = normalize_gripper_action(action, binarize=True)\\n\\n    # [OpenVLA] The dataloader flips the sign of the gripper action to align with other datasets\\n    # (0 = close, 1 = open), so flip it back (-1 = open, +1 = close) before executing the action\\n    if model_family == \\\"openvla\\\":\\n        action = invert_gripper_action(action)\\n\\n    return action\\n\\n\\ndef run_episode(\\n    cfg: GenerateConfig,\\n    env,\\n    task_description: str,\\n    model,\\n    resize_size,\\n    processor=None,\\n    action_head=None,\\n    proprio_projector=None,\\n    noisy_action_projector=None,\\n    initial_state=None,\\n    log_file=None,\\n):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Run a single episode in the environment.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    # For best performance (in terms of \\\"\\n               \\\"both speed and success rate), we recommend executing the full action chunk.\\\")\\n    action_queue = deque(maxlen=cfg.num_open_loop_steps)\\n\\n    # Get action from queue\\n            action = action_queue.popleft()\\n\\n            # Process action\\n            action = process_action(action, cfg.model_family)\\n\\n            # Execute action in environment\\n            obs, reward, done, info = env.step(action.tolist())\\n            if done:\\n                success = True\\n                break\\n            t += 1\\n\\n    except Exception as e:\\n        log_message(f\\\"Episode error: {e}\\\", log_file)\\n\\n    return success, replay_images\\n\\n\\ndef run_t\\n\\n... Note that unlike the other action\\n    dimensions, the gripper action is not normalized to [-1,+1] by default.\\n\\n    Normalization formula: y = 2 * (x - orig_low) / (orig_high - orig_low) - 1\\n\\n    Args:\\n        action: Action array with gripper action in the last dimension\\n        binarize: Whether to binarize gripper action to -1 or +1\\n\\n    Returns:\\n        np.ndarray: Action array with normalized gripper action\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    # Create a copy to avoid modifying the original\\n    normalized_action = action.copy()\\n\\n    # Normalize the last action dimension to [-1,+1]\\n    orig_low, orig_high = 0.0, 1.0\\n    normalized_action[..., -1] = 2 * (normalized_action[..., -1] - orig_low) / (orig_high - orig_low) - 1\\n\\n    if binarize:\\n        # Binarize to -1 or +1\\n        normalized_action[..., -1] = np.sign(normalized_action[..., -1])\\n\\n    return normalized_action\\n\\n\\ndef invert_gripper_action(action: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    Flip the sign of the gripper action (last dimension of action vector).\\n\\n    This is necessary for environments where -1 = open, +1 = close, since\\n    the RLDS dataloader aligns gripper actions such that 0 = close, 1 = open.\\n\\n    Args:\\n        action: Action array with gripper action in the last dimension\\n\\n    Returns:\\n        np.ndarray: Action array with inverted gripper action\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    # Create a copy to avoid modifying the original\\n    inverted_action = action.copy()\\n\\n    # Invert the gripper action\\n    inverted_action[..., -1] *= -1.0\\n\\n    return inverted_action\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.45}}"}

Process images\\n        all_images = prepare_images_for_vla(all_images, cfg)\\n\\n        # Extract primary image and additional images\\n        primary_image = all_images.pop(0)\\n\\n        # Build VLA prompt\\n        prompt = f\\\"In: What action should the robot take to {task_label.lower()}?\\\\nOut:\\\"\\n\\n        # Process primary image\\n        inputs = processor(prompt, primary_image).to(DEVICE, dtype=torch.bfloat16)\\n\\n        # Process additional wrist images if any\\n        if all_images:\\n            all_wrist_inputs = [\\n                processor(prompt, image_wrist).to(DEVICE, dtype=torch.bfloat16) for image_wrist in all_images\\n            ]\\n            # Concatenate all images\\n            primary_pixel_values = inputs[\\\"pixel_values\\\"]\\n            all_wrist_pixel_values = [wrist_inputs[\\\"pixel_values\\\"] for wrist_inputs in all_wrist_inputs]\\n            inputs[\\\"pixel_values\\\"] = torch.cat([primary_pixel_values] + all_wrist_pixel_values, dim=1)\\n\\n        # Process proprioception data if used\\n        proprio = None\\n        if cfg.use_proprio:\\n            proprio = obs[\\\"state\\\"]\\n            proprio_norm_stats = vla.norm_stats[cfg.unnorm_key][\\\"proprio\\\"]\\n            obs[\\\"state\\\"] = normalize_proprio(proprio, proprio_norm_stats)\\n            proprio = obs[\\\"state\\\"]\\n\\n        # Generate action\\n        if action_head is None:\\n            # Standard VLA output (single-image inputs, discrete actions)\\n            action, _ = vla.predict_action(**inputs, unnorm_key=cfg.unnorm_key, do_sample=False)\\n        else:\\n            # Custom action head for continuous actions\\n            action, _ = vla.predict_action(\\n                **inputs,\\n                unnorm_key=cfg.unnorm_key,\\n                do_sample=False,\\n                proprio=proprio,\\n                proprio_projector=proprio_projector,\\n                noisy_action_projector=noisy_action_projector,\\n                action_head=action_head,\\n                use_film=use_film,\\n            )\\n\\n    # Return action chunk as list of actions\\n    return [action[i] for i in range(len(action))]\\n\\n\\ndef get_action_from_server(\\n    observation: Dict[str, Any], server_endpoint: str = \\\"http://0.0.0.0:8777/act\\\"\\n) -> Dict[str, Any]:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    Get VLA action from remote inference server.\\n\\n    Args:\\n        observation: Observation data to send to server\\n        server_endpoint: URL of the inference server\\n\\n    Returns:\\n        Dict[str, Any]: Action response from server\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    response = requests.post(\\n        server_endpoint,\\n        json=observation,\\n    )\\n    return response.json()\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.52}}"}

Collect all input images\\n        all_images = [obs[\\\"full_image\\\"]]\\n        if cfg.num_images_in_input > 1:\\n            all_images.extend([obs[k] for k in obs.keys() if \\\"wrist\\\" in k])\\n\\n        # Process images\\n        all_images = prepare_images_for_vla(all_images, cfg)\\n\\n        # Extract primary image and additional images\\n        primary_image = all_images.pop(0)\\n\\n        # Build VLA prompt\\n        prompt = f\\\"In: What action should the robot take to {task_label.lower()}?\\\\nOut:\\\"\\n\\n        # Process primary image\\n        inputs = processor(prompt, primary_image).to(DEVICE, dtype=torch.bfloat16)\\n\\n        # Process additional wrist images if any\\n        if all_images:\\n            all_wrist_inputs = [\\n                processor(prompt, image_wrist).to(DEVICE, dtype=torch.bfloat16) for image_wrist in all_images\\n            ]\\n            # Concatenate all images\\n            primary_pixel_values = inputs[\\\"pixel_values\\\"]\\n            all_wrist_pixel_values = [wrist_inputs[\\\"pixel_values\\\"] for wrist_inputs in all_wrist_inputs]\\n            inputs[\\\"pixel_values\\\"] = torch.cat([primary_pixel_values] + all_wrist_pixel_values, dim=1)\\n\\n        # Process proprioception data if used\\n        proprio = None\\n        if cfg.use_proprio:\\n            proprio = obs[\\\"state\\\"]\\n            proprio_norm_stats = vla.norm_stats[cfg.unnorm_key][\\\"proprio\\\"]\\n            obs[\\\"state\\\"] = normalize_proprio(proprio, proprio_norm_stats)\\n            proprio = obs[\\\"state\\\"]\\n\\n        # Generate action\\n        if action_head is None:\\n            # Standard VLA output (single-image inputs, discrete actions)\\n            action, _ = vla.predict_action(**inputs, unnorm_key=cfg.unnorm_key, do_sample=False)\\n        else:\\n            # Custom action head for continuous actions\\n            action, _ = vla.predict_action(\\n                **inputs,\\n                unnorm_key=cfg.unnorm_key,\\n                do_sample=False,\\n                proprio=proprio,\\n                proprio_projector=proprio_projector,\\n                noisy_action_projector=noisy_action_projector,\\n                action_head=action_head,\\n                use_film=use_film,\\n            )\\n\\n    # Return action chunk as list of actions\\n    return [action[i] for i in range(len(action))]\\n\\n\\ndef get_action_from_server(\\n    observation: Dict[str, Any], server_endpoint: str = \\\"http://0.0.0.0:8777/act\\\"\\n) -> Dict[str, Any]:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    Get VLA action from remote inference server.\\n\\n    Args:\\n        observation: Observation data to send to server\\n        server_endpoint: URL of the inference server\\n\\n    Returns:\\n        Dict[str, Any]: Action response from server\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    response = requests.post(\\n        server_endpoint,\\n        json=observation,\\n    )\\n    return response.json()\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.52}}"

_source line 1072_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

````text
I found a critical difference!

Official `_load_dataset_stats`:
```python
vla.norm_stats = norm_stats  # REPLACES entirely
```

Our eval script:
```python
model.norm_stats.update(ds_stats)  # MERGES with existing
```

The official code **replaces** `model.norm_stats` entirely with the dataset_statistics.json contents. Our code **merges** (update) the new stats into the existing norm_stats, which already contains the 25 pre-training keys from config.json.

Could this matter? If the model's original norm_stats (from config.json) and the dataset_statistics.json have the same keys, the update would overwrite them. 

But here's the thing — the model starts with keys like "bridge_orig" and "fractal20220817_data" from config.json, while we're adding "libero_object_no_noops" from the dataset stats. Since there's no overlap, the merge just adds the new key without removing the old ones, so when `_unnormalize_actions` looks for the libero key, it finds it.

Actually, I'm wondering if there's something else going on. Let me reconsider the `run_task` function from the official eval — it loads initial states from a file using a specific key structure, but our code is fetching them directly from the benchmark object instead.
````

{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"def get_libero_env(task, model_family, resolution=256):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Initializes and returns the LIBERO environment, along with the task description.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    task_description = task.language\\n    task_bddl_file = os.path.join(get_libero_path(\\\"bddl_files\\\"), task.problem_folder, task.bddl_file)\\n    env_args = {\\\"bddl_file_name\\\": task_bddl_file, \\\"camera_heights\\\": resolution, \\\"camera_widths\\\": resolution}\\n    env = OffScreenRenderEnv(**env_args)\\n    env.seed(0)  # IMPORTANT: seed seems to affect object positions even when using fixed initial state\\n    return env, task_description\\n\\n\\ndef get_libero_dummy_action(model_family: str):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Get dummy/no-op action, used to roll out the simulation while the robot does nothing.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    return [0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, -1]\\n\\n\\ndef get_libero_image(obs):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Extracts third-person image from observations and preprocesses it.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    img = obs[\\\"agentview_image\\\"]\\n    img = img[::-1, ::-1]  # IMPORTANT: rotate 180 degrees to match train preprocessing\\n    return img\\n\\n\\ndef get_libero_wrist_image(obs):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Extracts wrist camera image from observations and preprocesses it.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    img = obs[\\\"robot0_eye_in_hand_image\\\"]\\n    img = img[::-1, ::-1]  # IMPORTANT: rotate 180 degrees to match train preprocessing\\n    return img\\n\\n\\ndef save_rollout_video(rollout_images, idx, success, task_description, log_file=None):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Saves an MP4 replay of an episode.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    rollout_dir = f\\\"./rollouts/{DATE}\\\"\\n    os.makedirs(rollout_dir, exist_ok=True)\\n    processed_task_description = task_description.lower().replace(\\\" \\\", \\\"_\\\").replace(\\\"\\\\n\\\", \\\"_\\\").replace(\\\".\\\", \\\"_\\\")[:50]\\n    mp4_path = f\\\"{rollout_dir}/{DATE_TIME}--openvla_oft--episode={idx}--success={success}--task={processed_task_description}.mp4\\\"\\n    video_writer = imageio.get_writer(mp4_path, fps=30)\\n    for img in rollout_images:\\n        video_writer.append_data(img)\\n    video_writer.close()\\n    print(f\\\"Saved rollout MP4 at path {mp4_path}\\\")\\n    if log_file is not None:\\n        log_file.write(f\\\"Saved rollout MP4 at path {mp4_path}\\\\n\\\")\\n    return mp4_path\\n\\n\\ndef quat2axisangle(quat):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    Copied from robosuite: https://github.com/ARISE-Initiative/robosuite/blob/eafb81f54ffc104f905ee48a16bb15f059176ad3/robosuite/utils/transform_utils.py#L490C1-L512C55\\n\\n    Converts quaternion to axis-angle format.\\n    Returns a unit vector direction scaled by its angle in radians.\\n\\n    Args:\\n        quat (np.array): (x,y,z,w) vec4 float angles\\n\\n    Returns:\\n        np.array: (ax,ay,az) axis-angle exponential coordinates\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    # clip quaternion\\n    if quat[3] > 1.0:\\n        quat[3] = 1.0\\n    elif quat[3] < -1.0:\\n        quat[3] = -1.0\\n\\n    den = np.sqrt(1.0 - quat[3] * quat[3])\\n    if math.isclose(den, 0.0):\\n        # This is (close to) a zero degree rotation, immediately return\\n        return np.zeros(3)\\n\\n    return (quat[:3] * 2.0 * math.acos(quat[3])) / den\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.44}}"}

}

The key difference is that `AutoModelForImageTextToText` got replaced with `AutoModelForVision2Seq`. Since my script is calling `AutoModelForVision2Seq.from_pretrained(...)`, and the config now maps that to the right class, the model loading should be working. When the official eval runs from the `./prismatic/` directory, the imports at the top of openvla_utils.py pull `OpenVLAForActionPrediction` directly from the repo code, not from the checkpoint. Then the registration call ties that class to the auto model. This means the official eval is using the repo's implementation, not whatever might be in the checkpoint.

But here's the critical part: when `from_pretrained` is called with `trust_remote_code=True`, HF checks the `auto_map` in config.json, which points to the checkpoint's own modeling file. So even though we've registered the class, `trust_remote_code=True` tells HF to load from the checkpoint directory instead of using the registered fallback. The class gets loaded from wherever the checkpoint's auto_map points to, not from our registration.

The previous session showed all 5 episodes failing with 290 steps, and the first 16 steps had the gripper stuck at -1.0 with large oscillating XYZ values.

The max steps for libero_object is 280, plus NUM_STEPS_WAIT=10, which totals 290—so the episode ran the full length without succeeding, which makes sense for a failure. But here's what's bothering me: the official eval reports ~90% success rate on libero_object, so with 5 episodes I'd expect 4-5 successes. Getting 0/5 is statistically unlikely (probability around 0.00001), which suggests something is genuinely wrong. Let me just run the official evaluation directly on this checkpoint to get a definitive answer.

Actually, I just noticed something critical in the dry-run output—it shows "large oscillating XYZ movements, Z especially extreme (up to -0.90)", but in my standalone test the actions were tiny (-0.02 to 0.02). That's a massive discrepancy. The first observation should be similar between the two setups, so why would the model produce such wildly different actions?

I'm wondering if the eval script is processing observations differently than the standalone test. Let me trace through both: the standalone test seeds with 42, resets, sets initial state, then immediately processes the agentview image with flips, resizing, and cropping. The eval script seeds with 0, resets, sets initial state, but then runs 10 dummy steps before processing the same image pipeline. That's the key difference—those dummy steps are changing the environment state before the model even sees its first real observation.

The seed difference (42 vs 0) might matter too, but the dummy steps are more suspicious. Let me add some diagnostics to the eval script to see what's actually happening: I'll print raw observation values like end-effector position at each step, log the raw action outputs before any processing, and save images so I can visually compare what the model is seeing in both cases. 1. `seed: int = 7` — the official eval uses seed 7, not 42!

3. `task_suite_name: str = TaskSuite.LIBERO_SPATIAL` — default is LIBERO_SPATIAL, they pass libero_object as arg

initial_state = np.array(all_initial_states[initial_states_task_key][episode_key]["initial_state"])

      "result": "{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"def run_task(\\n    cfg: GenerateConfig,\\n    task_suite,\\n    task_id: int,\\n    model,\\n    resize_size,\\n    processor=None,\\n    action_head=None,\\n    proprio_projector=None,\\n    noisy_action_projector=None,\\n    total_episodes=0,\\n    total_successes=0,\\n    log_file=None,\\n):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Run evaluation for a single task.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    # Get task\\n    task = task_suite.get_task(task_id)\\n\\n    # Get initial states\\n    initial_states, all_initial_states = load_initial_states(cfg, task_suite, task_id, log_file)\\n\\n    # Initialize environment and get task description\\n    env, task_description = get_libero_env(task, cfg.model_family, resolution=cfg.env_img_res)\\n\\n    # Start episodes\\n    task_episodes, task_successes = 0, 0\\n    for episode_idx in tqdm.tqdm(range(cfg.num_trials_per_task)):\\n        log_message(f\\\"\\\\nTask: {task_description}\\\", log_file)\\n\\n        # Handle initial state\\n        if cfg.initial_states_path == \\\"DEFAULT\\\":\\n            # Use default initial state\\n            initial_state = initial_states[episode_idx]\\n        else:\\n            # Get keys for fetching initial episode state from JSON\\n            initial_states_task_key = task_description.replace(\\\" \\\", \\\"_\\\")\\n            episode_key = f\\\"demo_{episode_idx}\\\"\\n\\n            # Skip episode if expert demonstration failed to complete the task\\n            if not all_initial_states[initial_states_task_key][episode_key][\\\"success\\\"]:\\n                log_message(f\\\"Skipping task {task_id} episode {episode_idx} due to failed expert demo!\\\", log_file)\\n                continue\\n\\n            # Get initial state\\n            initial_state = np.array(all_initial_states[initial_states_task_key][episode_key][\\\"initial_state\\\"])\\n\\n        log_message(f\\\"Starting episode {task_episodes + 1}...\\\", log_file)\\n\\n        # Run episode\\n        success, replay_images = run_episode(\\n            cfg,\\n            env,\\n            task_description,\\n            model,\\n            resize_size,\\n            processor,\\n            action_head,\\n            proprio_projector,\\n            noisy_action_projector,\\n            initial_state,\\n            log_file,\\n        )\\n\\n        # Update counters\\n        task_episodes += 1\\n        total_episodes += 1\\n        if success:\\n            task_successes += 1\\n            total_successes += 1\\n\\n        # Save replay video\\n        save_rollout_video(\\n            replay_images, total_episodes, success=success, task_description=task_description, log_file=log_file\\n        )\\n\\n        # Log results\\n        log_message(f\\\"Success: {success}\\\", log_file)\\n        log_message(f\\\"# episodes completed so far: {total_episodes}\\\", log_file)\\n        log_message(f\\\"# successes: {total_successes} ({total_successes / total_episodes * 100:.1f}%)\\\", log_file)\\n\\n    # Log task results\\n    task_success_rate = float(task_successes) / float(task_episodes) if task_episodes > 0 else 0\\n    total_success_rate = float(total_successes) / float(total_episodes) if total_episodes > 0 else 0\\n\\n    log_message(f\\\"Current task success rate: {task_success_rate}\\\", log_file)\\n    log_message(f\\\"Current total success rate: {total_success_rate}\\\", log_file)\\n\\n    # Log to wandb if enabled\\n    if cfg.use_wandb:\\n        wandb.log(\\n            {\\n                f\\\"success_rate/{task_description}\\\": task_success_rate,\\n                f\\\"num_episodes/{task_description}\\\": task_episodes,\\n            }\\n        )\\n\\n    return total_episodes, total_successes\\n\\n\\n@draccus.wrap()\\ndef eval_libero(cfg: GenerateConfig) -> float:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Main function to evaluate a trained policy on LIBERO benchmark tasks.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    # Validate configuration\\n    validate_config(cfg)\\n\\n    # Set random seed\\n    set_seed_everywhere(cfg.seed)\\n\\n    # Initialize model and components\\n    model, action_head, proprio_projector, noisy_action_projector, processor = initialize_model(cfg)\\n\\n    # Get expected image dimensions\\n    resize_size = get_image_resize_size(cfg)\\n\\n    # Setup logging\\n    log_file, local_log_filepath, run_id = setup_logging(cfg)\\n\\n    # Initialize LIBERO task suite\\n    benchmark_dict = benchmark.get_benchmark_dict()\\n    task_suite = benchmark_dict[cfg.task_suite_name]()\\n    num_tasks = task_suite.n_tasks\\n\\n    log_message(f\\\"Task suite: {cfg.task_suite_name}\\\", log_file)\\n\\n    # Start evaluation\\n    total_episodes, total_successes = 0, 0\\n    for task_id in tqdm.tqdm(range(num_tasks)):\\n        total_episodes, total_successes = run_task(\\n            cfg,\\n            task_suite,\\n            task_id,\\n            model,\\n            resize_size,\\n            processor,\\n            action_head,\\n            proprio_projector,\\n            noisy_action_projector,\\n            total_episodes,\\n            total_successes,\\n            log_file,\\n        )\\n\\n    # Calculate final success rate\\n    final_success_rate = float(total_successes) / float(total_episodes) if total_episodes > 0 else 0\\n\\n    # Log final results\\n    log_message(\\\"Final results:\\\", log_file)\\n    log_message(f\\\"Total episodes: {total_episodes}\\\", log_file)\\n    log_message(f\\\"Total successes: {total_successes}\\\", log_file)\\n    log_message(f\\\"Overall success rate: {final_success_rate:.4f} ({final_success_rate * 100:.1f}%)\\\", log_file)\\n\\n    # Log to wandb if enabled\\n    if cfg.use_wandb:\\n        wandb.log(\\n            {\\n                \\\"success_rate/total\\\": final_success_rate,\\n                \\\"num_episodes/total\\\": total_episodes,\\n            }\\n        )\\n        wandb.save(local_log_filepath)\\n\\n    # Close log file\\n    if log_file:\\n        log_file.close()\\n\\n    return final_success_rate\\n\\n\\nif __name__ == \\\"__main__\\\":\\n    eval_libero()\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.44}}"

For the main loop, I'm preparing observations, querying the model when the action queue is empty, processing actions, and stepping the environment forward.

Now I'm comparing this to our actual implementation: we seed everything at 42, load the vision-to-sequence model and merge in the dataset statistics, load the action head and proprioceptive projector, use 224 directly for the image size, and follow the same task loop structure with language and seeding. \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    Query the model to get action predictions.\\n\\n    Args:\\n        cfg: Configuration object with model parameters\\n        model: The loaded model\\n        obs: Observation proprio_projector: Optional proprioception projector\\n        noisy_action_projector: Optional noisy action projector for diffusion\\n        use_film: Whether to use FiLM\\n\\n    Returns:\\n        Union[List[np.ndarray], np.ndarray]: Predicted actions\\n\\n    Raises:\\n        ValueError: If model family is not supported\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    with torch.no_grad():\\n        if noisy_action_projector=noisy_action_projector,\\n                use_film=use_film,\\n            )\\n        else:\\n            raise ValueError(f\\\"Unsupported model family: {cfg.model_family}\\\")\\n\\n    return action\\n\\n\\ndef normalize_gripper_action(action: np.ndarray, binarize: bool = True) -> np.ndarray:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    Normalize gripper action from [0,1] to [-1,+1] range.\\n\\n    This is necessary for some environments because the dataset wrapper\\n    standardizes gripper actions to [0,1]. Note that unlike the other action\\n    dimensions, the gripper action is not normalized to [-1,+1] by default.\\n\\n    Normalization formula: y = 2 * (x - orig_low) / (orig_high - orig_low) - 1\\n\\n    Args:\\n        action: Action array with gripper action in the last dimension\\n        binarize: Whether to binarize gripper action to -1 or +1\\n\\n    Returns:\\n        np.ndarray: Action array with normalized gripper action\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    # Create a copy to avoid modifying the original\\n    normalized_action = action.copy()\\n\\n    # Normalize the last action dimension to [-1,+1]\\n    orig_low, orig_high = 0.0, 1.0\\n    normalized_action[..., -1] = 2 * (normalized_action[..., -1] - orig_low) / (orig_high - orig_low) - 1\\n\\n    if binarize:\\n        # Binarize to -1 or +1\\n        normalized_action[..., -1] = np.sign(normalized_action[..., -1])\\n\\n    return normalized_action\\n\\n\\ndef invert_gripper_action(action: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    Flip the sign of the gripper action (last dimension of action vector).\\n\\n    This is necessary for environments where -1 = open, +1 = close, since\\n    the RLDS dataloader aligns gripper actions such that 0 = close, 1 = open.\\n\\n    Args:\\n        action: Action array with gripper action in the last dimension\\n\\n    Returns:\\n        np.ndarray: Action array with inverted gripper action\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    # Create a copy to avoid modifying the original\\n    inverted_action = action.copy()\\n\\n    # Invert the gripper action\\n    inverted_action[..., -1] *= -1.0\\n\\n    return inverted_action\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.45}}"}

### Assistant

_source line 1190_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Interesting! The official OFT repo's `libero_requirements.txt` says `robosuite==1.4.1`, while the LIBERO-master `requirements.txt` says `robosuite==1.4.0`. We have `robosuite 1.4.1` installed. Also, `mujoco 3.8.1` is quite new.

But the OFT folks released their repo with robosuite 1.4.1, so that should be fine.

Let me check if the issue might be something else entirely. Let me think about whether the checkpoint was actually downloaded correctly. 

The safetensors shards look reasonable in size—around 5GB each for a total of 15GB, which matches what I'd expect for a 7B model in bf16 precision. The action_head and proprio_projector weights are also in the right ballpark. Let me verify the model actually loaded without errors and that the llm_dim parameter is set correctly, then run a quick inference to see if the outputs make sense compared to what the official repo would produce.

Actually, the real question is whether this task even works with any policy. The dummy actions ran fine at the start, and the episodes completed all 290 steps without crashing, so the environment itself is functional—the robot just isn't executing the right behaviors. Both our script and the official eval are getting 0%, the model loads and generates actions, but those actions aren't leading to task success. This could be a checkpoint mismatch, a LIBERO version incompatibility, or possibly corrupted safetensors files that downloaded incompletely.

Let me check the HuggingFace page for this model to see what success rate they report and what setup they're using, though I can't browse the web directly. I should also look into how the checkpoint was downloaded—if it came from HuggingFace, there might be metadata that tells me something.
```

OK, so the README shows `get_vla(cfg)` as the loading method, which we've already analyzed. It does `AutoModelForVision2Seq.from_pretrained()` without any explicit LoRA loading.

But wait — there IS a LoRA adapter in the checkpoint. Let me think about this more carefully.

In the `get_vla()` function, the model is loaded with `AutoModelForVision2Seq.from_pretrained(checkpoint)`. If the checkpoint's safetensors are the BASE model, and the LoRA adapter is separate, then the LoRA adapter needs to be loaded explicitly.


The official eval also gets 0%, and it doesn't load LoRA explicitly either. I'm wondering if recent transformers versions with PEFT support automatically detect and load LoRA adapters from `adapter_config.json`, but the adapter is nested in a `lora_adapter/` subdirectory rather than at the root, so `from_pretrained` probably wouldn't find it automatically.

Actually, I'm realizing the 982 weight keys in the safetensors might already contain LoRA-adjusted weights that were merged into the base model during saving — the weights would just be regular keys without "lora" in the names. So I need to figure out whether the safetensors were saved after merging LoRA or before. I can check by comparing a specific weight tensor against the base OpenVLA model to see if they differ, or look at the model size in `model.safetensors.index.json` and compare it to the base model.

Looking at the file sizes, the safetensors total around 15.08GB, which is very close to the expected 14GB for a 7B parameter model in bf16 — so the size alone doesn't reveal whether LoRA was merged, since merging doesn't change the parameter count, just modifies the existing weights.

Let me try a different angle: the adapter file is 484MB, which in bf16 corresponds to roughly 242M parameters — right in the expected range for LoRA with r=32 on all-linear targets for a 7B model. If the main model weights are still the base model without LoRA applied, then the adapter would be separate.

The fact that we're getting 0% success rate on LIBERO tasks suggests the base model is running without fine-tuning, which would happen if the safetensors are unmerged base weights and the LoRA adapter isn't being loaded. The real question is why the official evaluation code isn't loading the LoRA adapter — I need to check if there's a custom loading procedure in the OpenVLA repository that I'm missing. I'm wondering if the adapter needs to be in a specific directory structure for the newer transformers versions to auto-detect it during model loading.

```text
PEFT is installed but can't import because of a version incompatibility with transformers 4.40.1 (it needs `EncoderDecoderCache` which was added later). This means:

1. transformers 4.40.1 is installed
2. peft is installed but broken (incompatible version)

With transformers >= 4.37, `from_pretrained` CAN auto-load PEFT adapters if:
- There's an `adapter_config.json` in the model directory root
- OR if there's a `adapter_config.json` in a subdirectory like `lora_adapter/`


- But transformers 4.40 only checks the root directory for `adapter_config.json`, so a subdirectory placement wouldn't trigger auto-loading anyway. Even if it did, PEFT is broken and can't be imported, so the auto-loading would fail silently and the model would load without the adapter applied.

This points to the core issue: the base model weights are being used without the LoRA adapter merged in, resulting in 0% success rate. The solution is either fixing PEFT to a compatible version, manually merging the adapter into the base weights before saving, or properly loading both the model and adapter through PEFT. But I should verify whether the safetensors file actually contains merged weights or just the base model — maybe the `lora_adapter/` directory is just a training artifact and the real weights are already combined. Let me check if there's an `adapter_config.json` at the checkpoint root level rather than nested inside `lora_adapter/`.
```

Only in `lora_adapter/` subdirectory, not in the root. Transformers 4.40 auto-loads PEFT adapters if it finds `adapter_config.json` in the model root directory. Since it's only in a subdirectory, it won't auto-detect.

So the model is loaded WITHOUT the LoRA adapter. This is the root cause of the 0% SR.

But wait — the official eval ALSO doesn't explicitly load LoRA. And the official eval ALSO gets 0%. So maybe this is expected with this particular checkpoint when loaded this way?

Let me re-read the official code. In `get_vla()`, after loading the model:


The code conditionally applies LoRA through a FiLM wrapper that creates a LoRA configuration and wraps the model with it, but this only happens when FiLM is enabled.

Now I'm realizing that FiLM isn't actually used for LIBERO in OpenVLA-OFT since the config defaults to `use_film: False`. So I need to reconsider how the LoRA adapter gets loaded during evaluation. Let me trace through the actual training and saving procedure—when a PEFT model is saved with `model.save_pretrained()`, the behavior varies depending on the library version: sometimes it saves the full merged model, and sometimes just the adapter weights. If the safetensors checkpoint is the merged model, that would explain how evaluation works without explicitly loading an adapter.

But then I'm stuck on why we're getting 0% success rate if the merged weights should be functional. The `.aria2` files on the adapter directory suggest an incomplete download, though the main safetensors files appear complete. If the safetensors are actually the base model rather than the merged version, then we'd need the adapter to get any reasonable performance at all.

Let me check what the actual model weights contain by examining a sample tensor to determine whether the LoRA has been merged in or not.
```

{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"\\\"\\\"\\\"\\nLoads a checkpoint that only has a LoRA adapter (no merged model) and merges the adapter\\ninto the base OpenVLA model. Saves the final checkpoint in the same directory.\\n\\nMake sure to specify the correct base checkpoint when running this script. For example,\\n- if you fine-tuned the default OpenVLA-7B model without modifications, then `--base_checkpoint==\\\"openvla/openvla-7b\\\"`\\n- if you fine-tuned a different model or resumed fine-tuning from a different checkpoint, then specify that base checkpoint\\n- if you fine-tuned the default OpenVLA-7B model with modifications to `modeling_prismatic.py` (OpenVLA class definition),\\n  then the base checkpoint path should point to the checkpoint containing the modifications\\n\\nUsage:\\n    python vla-scripts/merge_lora_weights_and_save.py \\\\\\n        --base_checkpoint openvla/openvla-7b \\\\\\n        --lora_finetuned_checkpoint_dir /PATH/TO/CHECKPOINT/DIR/\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\nimport os\\nimport time\\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\nfrom typing import Union\\n\\nimport draccus\\nimport torch\\nfrom peft import PeftModel\\nfrom transformers import AutoConfig, AutoImageProcessor, AutoModelForVision2Seq, AutoProcessor\\n\\nfrom prismatic.extern.hf.configuration_prismatic import OpenVLAConfig\\nfrom prismatic.extern.hf.modeling_prismatic import OpenVLAForActionPrediction\\nfrom prismatic.extern.hf.processing_prismatic import PrismaticImageProcessor, PrismaticProcessor\\n\\n\\n@dataclass\\nclass ConvertConfig:\\n    # fmt: off\\n\\n    base_checkpoint: Union[str, Path] = \\\"\\\"                   # Base model checkpoint path/dir (either openvla/openvla-7b or whichever model you fine-tuned / resumed training from)\\n    lora_finetuned_checkpoint_dir: Union[str, Path] = \\\"\\\"     # Checkpoint directory containing the LoRA adapter\\n\\n    # fmt: on\\n\\n\\n@draccus.wrap()\\ndef main(cfg: ConvertConfig) -> None:\\n    # Register OpenVLA model to HF Auto Classes (not needed if the model is on HF Hub)\\n    AutoConfig.register(\\\"openvla\\\", OpenVLAConfig)\\n    AutoImageProcessor.register(OpenVLAConfig, PrismaticImageProcessor)\\n    AutoProcessor.register(OpenVLAConfig, PrismaticProcessor)\\n    AutoModelForVision2Seq.register(OpenVLAConfig, OpenVLAForActionPrediction)\\n\\n    # Load Model using HF AutoClasses\\n    print(f\\\"Loading base model: {cfg.base_checkpoint}\\\")\\n    vla = AutoModelForVision2Seq.from_pretrained(\\n        cfg.base_checkpoint,\\n        torch_dtype=torch.bfloat16,\\n        low_cpu_mem_usage=True,\\n        trust_remote_code=True,\\n    )\\n\\n    # Load LoRA weights and merge into base model, then save final checkpoint\\n    print(\\\"Merging LoRA weights into base model...\\\")\\n    start_time = time.time()\\n    merged_vla = PeftModel.from_pretrained(vla, os.path.join(cfg.lora_finetuned_checkpoint_dir, \\\"lora_adapter\\\")).to(\\n        \\\"cuda\\\"\\n    )\\n    merged_vla = merged_vla.merge_and_unload()\\n    merged_vla.save_pretrained(cfg.lora_finetuned_checkpoint_dir)\\n    print(f\\\"\\\\nMerging complete! Time elapsed (sec): {time.time() - start_time}\\\")\\n    print(f\\\"\\\\nSaved merged model checkpoint at:\\\\n{cfg.lora_finetuned_checkpoint_dir}\\\")\\n\\n\\nif __name__ == \\\"__main__\\\":\\n    main()\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.46}}"}
